# G1: SDN-AP against FFM-propagated Starlink and precise orbits (baselines)

Generated by `experiments/sdn-gp-starlink-ffm-match/tables.mjs` from recorded window rows. Position errors are 3D RMS in km unless a column says otherwise; every cell is a median over windows. Fit interval 24 h; held-out day k is (24 h + (k-1) d, 24 h + k d].

**Name.** SDN-AP ("advanced propagator", owner, 2026-10-24) is the new name of the theory first called SDN-GP. The module's package, methods and parameters change with it (`sdn-gp-propagator` to `sdn-ap-propagator`, `fit_sdngp` to `fit_sdnap`, `SDNGP_*` to `SDNAP_*`); builds before the rename are described by the names they carry, and `lib/sdngp.mjs` reads the renamed ones back under the historical keys, which the rows, the paths (`rows/sdngp/<build>/`), the experiment directory and the graph task keep. From build b9bc708baa50 the two fit methods are one, `fit`, and every method of the module takes a required $PCF `configuration` port (SDS 1.264.0) whose THEORY selects SGP4XP or SDNAP; `lib/pcf.mjs` encodes it (THEORY SDNAP, every other field at its documented default) and the adapters send it to the builds that declare the port.

## Method in brief

- **Truth.** Starlink: the satellite's SpaceX forecast is fitted over 0-48 h (never across the 48 h join) by `analysis/od fit_ffm` (FFM-1, NRLMSISE-00 drag with s = 0 because no $DCL is published yet, kappa x drag folded into B_eff, Cr*A/m locked), then FFM-1 propagates the fitted state for 96 h at 60 s. That trajectory is the truth of the `FFM-propagated truth` row; the `SpaceX forecast` row fits the same two theories to SpaceX's own states (24 h) and scores day 1 inside its span, strictly before the 48 h join. Precise orbits: ESA final GNSS, GFZ MGEX rapid GEO and IGSO satellites (2025), GFZ rapid GNSS orbits of 2026-10-11 to 10-22, ILRS combined weekly and NSGF arcs (the archive's and, for September and October 2026, those fetched from NASA CDDIS), NASA ISS OEM, windows as in the sgp4-xp measurement.
- **Windows.** 24 h fit from the first state; SGP4 by `fit_elements` (six mean elements and B*), SGP4-XP by `propagator/sgp4-xp fit` (those and BTERM, AGOM). One window per Starlink; the other sources use the strides of `config.json`.
- **SDN-AP.** Side B's module `sdn-ap-propagator` (theory 16; the package was `sdn-gp-propagator` through build 35cd83a2b714), used as built and by its published surfaces only: `fit` with a $PCF configuration whose THEORY is SDNAP (`fit_sdnap` from the rename to build 61d4ec4e6a12, `fit_sdngp` through build 35cd83a2b714) fits the six elements, a signed effective ballistic term B_eff, AGOM and the two density time-variation terms to the same 24 h of truth (the truth as a plain $OEM naming its frame; the run's saved space-weather table; no density correction and no prior), and the batch entry `sdnap_propagate_minutes_soa` (`sdngp_` through build 35cd83a2b714) propagates the fitted set to the held-out epochs (TEME), which `foundation/frames` takes to the truth's axes before the same `pair_statistics` scoring as XP; the sets the build fits as Moon-centered or segment sets (sections 14 and 15, and HEO TESS) are propagated by `propagate` on a grid block that names the set's own center, one explicit epoch per scored state, in the ICRF axes the module answers in. The module is rebuilt in place by its author, so every row belongs to a build named by the SHA-256 of its wasm (section 2a); the cells above are build b9bc708baa50. Scoring check: over the 1111 windows with three comparable held-out days the pair_statistics RMS of the days agrees with the module's own held-out RMS (its COMMENT) to a ratio of 0.825 to 1.134 (median 0.998). SpaceX's own forecast could be fitted only for the same-day capture: the server's file had changed since the registered fit for 199 of the registered seats, and a changed file is not the file the truth was fitted to.
- **RIC.** SGP4: `element_residuals` (RTN of the truth). SGP4-XP: the module's own fit split for the fit, and the fitted set propagated by the module and compared by `pair_statistics` for the held-out days (at most one state per 5 minutes). Components are the median over windows of each window's component RMS, so they do not sum exactly to the 3D median.
- **RIC check.** Over all held-out days of all windows the three components recombine to the module's own 3D RMS to 0.8643 to 1.2257 for SGP4-XP (the thinning to one state per 5 minutes) and 1.0000 to 1.0000 for SGP4.
- **Environment.** Earth orientation: the local IERS daily finals of 2026-10-13. Space weather: the `data-source/space-weather` rows (GFZ, NOAA SWPC) through 2026-10-25, then NOAA SWPC 45-day daily Ap and F10.7 for the eight days after (Kp and the 81-day average held), because the module's rows end where its Kp forecast ends and the truth runs 96 h. Planetary kernel DE440s. Density correction s = 0 (no $DCL is published yet). Hashes are in `manifest.json`.
- **COMSPOC XP (local scoring only).** Their published SGP4-XP sets, propagated through the black-box module and compared with the same-day truth; their set epochs are 1 to 5 days older than the truth, so their errors are at those ages, not at the 24 h of the fitted arms. Never fitted to, never an input; aggregates only.
- **Plan.** `experiments/sdn-gp-starlink-ffm-match/PLAN.md`, frozen by its first commit, with amendments A1 (headline table), A2 (start states, weather extension, the COMSPOC scoring arm), A3 (fit length), A4 (GEO and IGSO windows from the GFZ MGEX set found after registration), A5 (spacecraft truth from NAIF: highly elliptical orbits scored; the cislunar, lunar and heliocentric packs prepared, and scored from A9f on) A6 (truth quality: the iteration cap and the zero bound) A7 to A7d (the LEO fit floor against zonal-only gravity, DESIGN.md M1 to M3: section 12) A8 to A8c (current GNSS truth from the GFZ rapid orbits, TESS from MAST, CAPSTONE, Artemis I and Danuri from JPL Horizons, windows prepared for SDN-AP: section 1 and section 13) and A9 to A9h (Psyche, Lucy and Juno from Horizons, current laser-ranging truth from the ILRS products on CDDIS, the SDN-AP arm and its rules, its fit-length arm, the lowering variants, the fit span against COMSPOC, the module's default fit span, the Moon-centered windows and the segment fits of the cislunar and heliocentric packs).
- **Not measured here.** SDN-AP on Jupiter-centered orbits (the build documents no such path; every window is attempted and refused by name: section 15); SDN-AP's covariance (section 5); the NAIF kernels of Psyche, Lucy and Juno are SPK type 1, unreadable by `files/orbit-products` 0.1.4, so that truth comes from JPL Horizons vector tables; GEO operator ephemerides (SES, Intelsat) are not stored; the theory-term ranking (phase 3).

## 1. Headline: SDN-AP, best-case SGP4-XP, COMSPOC XP and SGP4, one row per regime

Cells read `SDN-AP / best-case XP / COMSPOC XP (local scoring only) / SGP4`. SDN-AP is side B's module `sdn-ap-propagator` as built (build b9bc708baa50: source commit 610930cb, 8 uncommitted file(s); the newest build that ran every source, see section 2a), fitted by `fit` to the same truth on the same windows; `pending` where it has not run. Best-case XP is `propagator/sgp4-xp fit` fitted to the same truth on the same windows. `-` is not scored. A COMSPOC value marked `*` is its median over the objects it could be scored on (same-day truth only); section 1b gives XP and SGP4 on exactly those objects. A COMSPOC value marked `†` (the GFZ rapid-orbit and laser-ranged regimes) is by days since the epoch of the set itself, day 1 being the first 24 h after it, against the rapid orbit; XP and SGP4 are by days since the end of their 24 h fit, so the ages are not the same (no COMSPOC fit span is known). For COMSPOC the Fit RMS cell is its error over the first 24 h of the truth: nothing is fitted to it.

### 1c. Fit span against COMSPOC: SDN-AP, SGP4-XP and SGP4 fitted over 24, 48, 72 and 120 h ending at the epoch of each COMSPOC set (amendment A9e)

COMSPOC does not state the span its XP sets were fitted over, so the 24 h fits of the table above are not a fair partner for them. Here every row holds the sets of one group whose precise-orbit truth covers the fit span (90 %) and the days after it; the window of each set ends at that set's epoch (only the epoch is used, nothing of the set enters a fit), and day k is days k-1 to k since it, the days COMSPOC is scored on. Cells read `SDN-AP / best-case XP / COMSPOC XP (local scoring only) / SGP4`, medians in km over the sets of the row; the COMSPOC value is over the same sets. Sets: the number of windows at that span.

### What the table says (generated from the numbers above)

- The in-track component carries 99.4 to 99.9 % of the day-3 squared error of SGP4-XP in the LEO regimes, 88.4 to 97.1 % in MEO and 86.6 to 90.9 % in IGSO and GEO: the terms that can buy accuracy are the along-track ones (drag and thrust, secular rate, time variation of density).
- The fit itself: in LEO XP and SGP4 fit alike (median RMS ratio 0.97 to 1.07, about 0.48 km, a common floor, which points at short-period terms that both theories leave out); where the orbit is high XP fits much tighter than SGP4: MEO, GPS / GLONASS / Galileo (ESA final) 5.4 times (0.022 against 0.122 km); IGSO, BeiDou-2 and QZSS-2 (GFZ MGEX rapid) 6.5 times (0.109 against 0.758 km); GEO, BeiDou-2 and QZSS-3 (GFZ MGEX rapid) 6.3 times (0.146 against 0.931 km); HEO, THEMIS A, D, E and Chandra (NAIF SPK), 24 h fit 76.1 times (0.109 against 8.271 km); HEO, TESS (13.7 day orbit; MAST definitive ephemeris), 24 h fit 124 times (0.207 against 25.7 km); MEO, GPS / GLONASS / Galileo / BeiDou (GFZ rapid, 2026-10-11 to 2026-10-22) 5.4 times (0.021 against 0.116 km); IGSO, BeiDou and QZSS (GFZ rapid, 2026-10-11 to 2026-10-22) 6.9 times (0.103 against 0.717 km); GEO, BeiDou (GFZ rapid, 2026-10-11 to 2026-10-22) 9.3 times (0.090 against 0.835 km).

QZSS (4 satellites, GFZ rapid orbits): of 33 outages 13 are scoreable (3 after the last day of the product, 2 a day without states of the satellite, 1 no states, 14 of a satellite without an orbit series); 13 of 13 have a detection inside the forecast window plus or minus 6 h and 0 of 13 inside the actual interval, but the detector reports a burn every 1.5 days (241 false alarms per object-year, delta-v median 1.8 mm/s), so a window without a notice holds one almost as often: 11.8 of 13 outages are expected detected at the pooled off-notice rate (probability of 13 or more 0.30); the geostationary satellite 11 of 11 against 11.0 expected.

- Controlled deorbits (section 16c; 19 lowering Starlinks, build b9bc708baa50): `reentry` on the 24 h fit answered for 19 (none refused; predicted lifetime median 12.4 days, 4.7 to 26.2); the rule setting (B_eff replaced by tau0) is refused for 0 of 19 (none); the free-B_eff setting gives the same epochs as the shipped one; as of the archive's newest snapshot 0 have a TIP message and 0 a decay message, so none is scored yet.
- First fits of the captured batch (section 16c; 27 satellites, held-out days 1 / 2 / 3 inside the file, median km): SDN-AP 6 h 1.5 / 6.3 / 14.9; 12 h 1.4 / 6.8 / 17.5; 24 h 0.3 / 1.3 / 2.7; SGP4-XP 6 h 122 / 604 / 1516; 12 h 154 / 687 / 1654; 24 h 7.7 / 21.3 / 22.6; the SDN-AP B_eff at 6 h over its 24 h value has a median of 1.01 (10th to 90th percentile 1.01 to 1.01).
- Batch-joint fit against per-object fits on synthetic FFM-1 batches with an exact parent state (section 16c, amendment A12b; build b9bc708baa50): separation sigma 1 m/s, 54 objects: 6 h joint 0.8 / 5.6 / 16.1 against per-object 3.5 / 19.1 / 51.7 km; 12 h joint 1.5 / 6.7 / 16.6 against per-object 1.9 / 8.7 / 22.0 km; 24 h joint 0.6 / 1.9 / 4.0 (1 of 2 calls answered; refused: fit-failed 1) against per-object 0.9 / 3.6 / 8.3 km. separation sigma 0.25 m/s, 54 objects: 6 h joint 1.8 / 8.6 / 21.8 against per-object 2.0 / 9.5 / 23.8 km; 12 h joint 0.4 / 1.9 / 5.2 against per-object 0.4 / 1.6 / 4.5 km; 24 h joint refused (0 of 2 calls answered; refused: fit-failed 2) against per-object 1.3 / 4.9 / 11.5 km.
- Moon-centered windows against the truth-side maneuver list (section 16b): LRO: with a listed maneuver in the fit span 2 windows (whole 1, segment 1, refused 0), none in the fit span 85 (segment 4, refused 0); segment starts 1 after and 0 before the last listed maneuver; Danuri: with a listed maneuver in the fit span 14 windows (whole 7, segment 6, refused 1), none in the fit span 47 (segment 2, refused 0); segment starts 5 after and 1 before the last listed maneuver.

### 2c. The module's default fit span against the registered fit, same windows (amendment A9f)

The block holds the truth from 120 h before the fit end and declares only the end of the fit; the module chooses the span (its rule: the latest 48 h, extended to 72 and then 120 h while the fit RMS holds; the whole interval when the truth is shorter). Held-out days as before. Element bytes are the size-prefixed `$OMM` record holding only the mean elements and the drag (and solar) terms of the set; the record as returned carries the fit statistics too. A two-line text set (the format COMSPOC publishes its XP sets in) is 138 characters.

One propagated state, microseconds (one `propagate` call over 5761 states, four days at 60 s, median of repeats, harness overhead included):

| Regime | Theory | us per state | Element bytes |
| --- | --- | --: | --: |
| LEO (ISS) | SGP4 | 257 | 304 |
| LEO (ISS) | SGP4-XP | 290 | 312 |
| MEO (GPS) | SGP4 | 288 | 304 |
| MEO (GPS) | SGP4-XP | 329 | 312 |
| GEO (BeiDou-2 GEO) | SGP4 | 331 | 304 |
| GEO (BeiDou-2 GEO) | SGP4-XP | 289 | 320 |

Fitted terms (medians over windows): the two extra terms of SGP4-XP against the single B* of SGP4. AGOM is the solar-radiation term; a zero median means the fit left it at zero in most windows.

## 5. Covariance realism against the FFM truth of the same-day Starlinks

d^2 is the position Mahalanobis distance of the error against each source's own covariance (gp-error-model `pair_statistics`); a realistic covariance has mean d^2/3 in [0.8, 1.25] and 95 % coverage in [0.93, 0.97] (the V1c2 rule). Age is in days since the source's own epoch: ScenarioEpoch for SynCoPate, the fit epoch for ours.

## 7. The Starlink truth set

Fitted 239 satellites (199 registered seats, 40 from the same-day local capture); 0 failures. fit_ffm against SpaceX over 0-48 h: RMS median 0.431 km, mean 1.944, 90th percentile 1.811. B_eff at the zero bound: 52; not converged: 56; with detected maneuver events: 0. Median fit CPU 77.2 s. SGP4-XP fits the module flags as not converged are kept in the tables: 2 of 239 on the FFM truth, 7 of 239 on the SpaceX states.

Fitted by stratum, all populations: alt0-inc0: 13, alt0-inc1: 16, alt0-inc2: 7, alt0-inc3: 4, alt1-inc0: 3, alt1-inc1: 70, alt1-inc2: 14, alt1-inc3: 29, alt2-inc0: 50, alt2-inc1: 4, alt2-inc2: 7, alt2-inc3: 1, alt3-inc0: 1, alt3-inc1: 6, alt3-inc2: 13, alt3-inc3: 1.

Registered seats fitted / allocated by stratum: alt0-inc0: 13 / 13, alt0-inc1: 15 / 15, alt0-inc2: 7 / 7, alt0-inc3: 4 / 4, alt1-inc0: 3 / 3, alt1-inc1: 48 / 48, alt1-inc2: 13 / 14, alt1-inc3: 27 / 27, alt2-inc0: 40 / 40, alt2-inc1: 4 / 4, alt2-inc2: 6 / 6, alt2-inc3: 1 / 1, alt3-inc0: 1 / 1, alt3-inc1: 4 / 4, alt3-inc2: 12 / 12, alt3-inc3: 1 / 1. The seats were drawn round-robin over the strata and drag terciles, so a partial run covers every stratum before it deepens any.

Truth inventory by regime: [inventory.md](inventory.md).

## 9. Does the zero bound or the iteration cap bias the FFM-Starlink truth? (amendment A6)

Fit RMS is the fit of FFM-1 to SpaceX's own forecast over 0-48 h, the measure of how well the truth reproduces the operator. The XP and SGP4 columns are the median day-3 errors against that truth (km).

| Group | Fits | B_eff median (m2/kg) | Fit RMS vs SpaceX: median / 90th / max (km) | XP day 3 | SGP4 day 3 |
| --- | --: | --: | --- | --: | --: |
| at the 12-iteration cap; B_eff 0 to 0.5 | 36 | 0.0184 | 0.319 / 0.935 / 19.5 | 9.831 | 16.4 |
| at the 12-iteration cap; B_eff above 0.5 | 9 | 1.7450 | 11.4 / 19.1 / 21.1 | 28.0 | 19.2 |
| at the 12-iteration cap; B_eff at zero | 11 | 0.0000 | 0.552 / 0.907 / 165 | 3.841 | 16.5 |
| converged; B_eff 0 to 0.5 | 132 | 0.0120 | 0.268 / 0.728 / 11.7 | 9.817 | 13.7 |
| converged; B_eff above 0.5 | 10 | 1.6232 | 2.445 / 8.162 / 13.2 | 17.9 | 45.8 |
| converged; B_eff at zero | 41 | 0.0000 | 0.583 / 1.308 / 32.0 | 3.704 | 14.5 |

Refit of the fits that stopped at the registered 12 iterations, on the same SpaceX file (SHA-256 checked), cap 30 (47 seat files had changed on SpaceX's server and are in the next paragraph): 9 satellites; 8 of 9 converge within 30 iterations (median 16). The truth moves by (median / maximum, km): 0.000 / 4.350 at 24 h, 0.000 / 1.159 at 48 h, 0.000 / 19.9 at 72 h, 0.000 / 53.7 at 96 h. Moved by more than 0.1 km at 96 h: 48129 (fit RMS 21.1 km, moved 53.7 km). B_eff changes by a median 0.0 %; the fit RMS against SpaceX goes from 0.277 to 0.277 km (median); the median XP day-3 error against the truth goes from 12.9 to 12.9 km; 0 left the zero bound and 0 entered it.

Seats whose file had changed: cap 12 and cap 30 both run on the file served now, only the pairs where the cap-12 fit did not converge: 7 satellites; 5 of 7 converge within 30 iterations (median 16). The truth moves by (median / maximum, km): 0.000 / 58.2 at 24 h, 0.000 / 123 at 48 h, 0.000 / 251 at 72 h, 0.000 / 326 at 96 h. Moved by more than 0.1 km at 96 h: 101011 (fit RMS 290 km, moved 326 km), 67211 (fit RMS 26.6 km, moved 57.8 km). B_eff changes by a median 0.0 %; the fit RMS against SpaceX goes from 1.776 to 1.776 km (median); the median XP day-3 error against the truth goes from 12.8 to 12.8 km; 0 left the zero bound and 0 entered it.

Effect on the tables (SGP4-XP, day 1 / day 3, median and 90th percentile, km): all 239 truths 1.502 / 9.867 and 4.059 / 23.2; the 132 clean ones 1.456 / 9.817 and 2.973 / 22.1; the 52 at the zero bound 1.018 / 3.768; the 19 above 0.5 m2/kg 13.3 / 25.4. The two limits pull in opposite directions (drag-free truths are easy, decaying ones are hard), so the pooled median moves little while the spread does.

How to read it, in numbers: the registered rows keep the 12-iteration truth. The `clean` row of section 1b (converged, B_eff between 0 and 0.5) is the population on which the tables are least affected by the two limits; compare its day-1 and day-3 medians with the pooled Starlink row. The zero bound is a contract of the T1a module (`analysis/od fit_ffm`, B_eff >= 0): a satellite whose forecast contains net thrust beyond its drag gets a drag-free trajectory, so its truth is easy to match (lower XP and SGP4 errors) and does not follow the operator; those 52 fits are reported apart, not corrected here.

## 10. Held out against the first hours of newer SpaceX files

239 fitted Starlinks (478 scorings), each scored against the first three hours (every ten minutes) of a file SpaceX served after the file of the registered fit; the newest starts 2026-10-24T12:05Z. The horizon from the start of the registered fit to those hours is 5.4 to 24.9 h; the scoring is re-run with `newer.mjs` as files arrive (the registered V2 prospective files start 2026-10-24 08:00Z, a horizon of about 20 h), and each pass is reported at its own horizon. Arms: SpaceX's own earlier forecast (where its epochs coincide with the newer file's), FFM-1 from the registered fit, SGP4, SGP4-XP and SDN-AP (the newest build that ran every source, section 2a; `newer.mjs` uses it when one exists) fitted to the first 24 h of the FFM truth, and COMSPOC's XP set (same-day objects, local scoring only).

### COMSPOC XP sets of the GNSS and laser-ranged objects against precise orbits (local scoring only)

91 COMSPOC XP sets are in the local capture of 2026-10-23T20:09Z; 43 of those objects have precise-orbit products in the archive (32 GNSS, 11 laser-ranged). **GNSS:** 31 sets (epochs 2026-10-14 to 2026-10-20) are scored against the GFZ rapid orbits of 2026-10-11 to 2026-10-22 (amendment A8; the days from 2026-10-20 use Bulletin A predicted Earth orientation, under 0.15 m). **Laser-ranged:** 7 of the 11 sets are scored against the NSGF rapid orbits of the LEO spheres fetched from NASA CDDIS (daily files to 2026-10-24; amendments A9 and A9b); 4 wait (NORAD 19751, epoch 2026-10-19; 20026, epoch 2026-10-19; 22195, epoch 2026-10-20; 8820, epoch 2026-10-20) because the newest ILRS combined weekly orbits on CDDIS end 2026-10-03. 1 GNSS set(s) wait (NORAD 62339, epoch 2026-10-17, not in the rapid products). Re-run `comspoc-precise.mjs` as products appear. The ESA final orbits end 2026-09-23.

The error does not grow over the days after the epoch and is mostly in-track (components above): a set that sits off the orbit by a near-constant amount, not one that drifts. That is the form of the fit floor of section 12 (the tesseral in-track variation a mean-element set cannot follow); the best-case XP fit RMS of the LEO sphere row of section 1 is 0.406 km.

1 set(s) are far from the rapid orbit on every day (NORAD 68791, epoch 2026-10-14, 30244 km in the first day): the set describes another orbit than the product does (a satellite being repositioned, or a stale set). The medians are over all sets and are not moved by it. The median error does not rise over the first three days after the epoch; the best-case XP of the headline rows is fitted to the 24 h of the rapid orbit just before the days it is scored on, so the two are not like for like.

## 11. Notes for the modules (found here, not changed here)

- `analysis/od fit_ffm` (T1a): (a) B_eff >= 0 leaves 52 of 239 fits at the zero bound (orbit raising and other net thrust cannot be represented; their truth is a drag-free trajectory); (b) 56 of 239 fits stop at the 12-iteration default; refitted with a cap of 30 on 16 of them (8 of 9 converge and 5 of 7 converge, about 16.0 iterations), the truth moves by a median of at most 0.000 km at 96 h and, for the 11 fits that reproduce SpaceX to 2 km, by at most 0.000 km, so the stopping rule or the default cap is too strict; (c) 19 objects need B_eff above 0.5 m2/kg, more than ten times a physical value (continuous lowering or decay, no discrete event detected): they fit SpaceX to a median of 2 to 11 km and are 2 km (median) from a newer file's first hours; a single B_eff cannot represent them.
- `data-source/space-weather` rows end where the Kp forecast ends (about two days ahead) and `propagator/ffm` refuses any arc past them (`space-weather-out-of-range`); this experiment appended NOAA SWPC 45-day Ap and F10.7 itself (amendment A2).
- `files/orbit-products` 0.1.4 reads SPK types 8, 9 and 13 only: the JPL navigation kernels of Psyche, Lucy and Juno are type 1 (`unsupported-variant`), the Juno merge also holds type 2 (`unsupported-spk-materialization`), TDRSS is type 10 (generated from two-line elements). Its epochs are TDB, so every caller needs `foundation/time` (done here, one call per epoch).
- `sdn-gp-propagator` (side B), found by using it. Fixed in later builds: the invoke `propagate` once stopped one day after a set's epoch, and `fit_sdngp` once read a block that named GCRF only through the enumeration as TEME, silently (30 to 40 km errors); frames are now read or refused by name and `propagate` takes a grid. Still true of the headline build: (a) the build is rebuilt in place from a working tree, so a result is reproducible only with the copy of the wasm it ran (section 2a); (b) `ingest_omm` and the batch entry refuse a segment set (`unsupported-theory`, documented), so a consumer reaches it only through `propagate` on a grid; (c) the COMMENT of a segment set says `N states fitted from X h`, where X is the offset of the segment within the arc (the epoch carried past a maneuver), which reads like a length.
- Rule 2c.2 of the module (a B_eff above 0.5 m2/kg is replaced by tau0, held after the arc) on the 19 Starlinks with such a B_eff (section 2b): the rule gives day 1 / day 3 median 0.4 / 4.1 km, free B_eff day 1 / day 3 median 0.4 / 4.1 km, SGP4-XP day 1 / day 3 median 13.3 / 25.4 km, SGP4 day 1 / day 3 median 10.3 / 45.2 km; the build as shipped gave day 1 / day 3 median 0.4 / 4.1 km (rule applied to 0 of 19).
- GNSS MEO (GFZ rapid), held-out day 3 median: SDN-AP 0.128 km, SGP4-XP 0.118 km (fit RMS 0.013 against 0.021 km): SDN-AP is behind XP in extrapolation at this altitude although it fits tighter.
- SDN-AP fits that did not converge or were refused, by regime: GEO, BeiDou-2 and QZSS-3 (GFZ MGEX rapid): 6 of 90; IGSO, BeiDou and QZSS (GFZ rapid, 2026-10-11 to 2026-10-22): 1 of 30; Moon-centered, Danuri (JPL Horizons, 130 km, perilune 60 km), 24 h fit: 1 of 31; Cislunar, Artemis II after the translunar burn (NAIF SPK, Earth-centered), 24 h fit (segment fits: section 15): 1 of 3; Jupiter-centered, Juno orbits (JPL Horizons), 66 d fit: 19 of 19; HEO, Artemis II high-Earth-orbit checkout (one short arc: 8 h fit, 12.85 h held out as day 1): 1 of 1.

## 12. The LEO fit floor against zonal-only gravity (DESIGN.md M1 to M3)

Plan amendments A7 to A7d. Each window is one start state: 132 clean Starlink truths (converged, 0 < B_eff <= 0.5 m2/kg) at their registered start states, 12 ISS windows (the registered NASA OEM, state turned EME2000 to GCRF), 12 LAGEOS-1 and LAGEOS-2 windows (first state of the weekly ILRS orbits of 2026-07-19 to 2026-08-23, drag off). FFM-1 propagates it for 120 h at 60 s under EGM2008 70x70 (`full`) and under FFM's own truncations: `zonal` (order 0, the zonals to degree 70), `tess2` and `tess6` (zonals plus the tesseral and sectorial terms to degree 2 or 6), `order14` and `order15` (orders up to 14 and 15; their difference is the 15th order alone), and, for Starlink and the ISS, `full-nodrag` and `zonal-nodrag` (drag off). Everything else in the force model (third bodies, solid and ocean tides, relativity, Earth radiation, SRP, and drag where it is on) is the same in every variant, so a difference between two variants is the gravity terms that were dropped. Every variant is fitted by SGP4 and SGP4-XP over 24 h, 21.7 h and 34.2 h and scored on the three held-out days as in section 1; the difference series between variants are taken by `pair_statistics` and summarized by a quadratic plus one least-squares sinusoid. The `full` re-propagation reproduces the registered truth to 0.0e+0 km (largest difference over 132 Starlinks).

### 12g. What it means for the fit

- **The floor is gravity.** In-track fit RMS of best-case XP with the full field against the zonal field only: Starlink 48 to 60 deg (53 deg shell) 401 against 8 m; ISS (51.64 deg) 406 against 9 m; LAGEOS-1 (109.84 deg) 119 against 2 m. The rest of the force model is the same in both, so the difference is the tesseral and sectorial terms and nothing else.
- **Day 3 is gravity.** The held-out day-3 error of the same fits, full field against zonal only: Starlink 48 to 60 deg (53 deg shell) XP 8.08 against 0.61 km, SGP4 11.32 against 0.63 km; ISS (51.64 deg) XP 11.65 against 0.56 km, SGP4 15.75 against 0.58 km; LAGEOS-1 (109.84 deg) XP 0.36 against 0.03 km, SGP4 1.10 against 0.06 km. The zonal-only values are the ceiling for a theory that carries the m-daily terms and fits the same 24 h.
- **J22 alone.** Degree-2 tesserals only (`tess2`), XP day 3 at 24 h: Starlink 48 to 60 deg (53 deg shell) 6.25 km; ISS (51.64 deg) 7.83 km; LAGEOS-1 (109.84 deg) 0.43 km.
- **A longer fit, no new physics.** XP day 3 at 24 h against 34.2 h (full field): Starlink 48 to 60 deg (53 deg shell) 8.08 against 4.78 km; ISS (51.64 deg) 11.65 against 5.90 km; LAGEOS-1 (109.84 deg) 0.36 against 0.27 km; SGP4: Starlink 48 to 60 deg (53 deg shell) 11.32 against 5.39 km; ISS (51.64 deg) 15.75 against 6.18 km; LAGEOS-1 (109.84 deg) 1.10 against 0.96 km. At 21.7 h XP has no drag term, so the same change is large only where the truth has no drag (`full-nodrag`) and is a loss where it has.

### 12h. Decision rules of A7b

- **Rule 1, floor (holds).** Tesseral share of the XP in-track fit RMS against the predicted floor: Starlink 48 to 60 deg (53 deg shell) 401 m against 384 m (1.04, 54 windows, within 25 %); ISS (51.64 deg) 406 m against 394 m (1.03, 12 windows, within 25 %); LAGEOS-1 (109.84 deg) 119 m against 110 m (1.08, 6 windows, within 25 %).
- **Rule 2, absorption (fails).** Median |BTERM| of XP on the drag-free truths, full against zonal: Starlink, all clean truths 8.62e-4 against 6.78e-7 (at least 3 times); ISS (51.64 deg) 0.00e+0 against 0.00e+0 (less than 3 times). SGP4 day 3 with B* fixed at 0 against B* fitted: Starlink, all clean truths full-nodrag 1.80 against 12.54 km (lower); ISS (51.64 deg) full-nodrag 1.30 against 16.08 km (lower); LAGEOS-1 (109.84 deg) full 0.39 against 1.10 km (lower); LAGEOS-2 (52.66 deg) full 0.44 against 2.22 km (lower).
- **Rule 3, fit length (holds).** J22 share of the XP day-3 error, 24 h to 21.7 h: Starlink 48 to 60 deg (53 deg shell) 6.22 to 0.00 km (-100 %; 34.2 h 0.77 km, -88 %; at least 25 % lower; at 21.7 h the tess2 and zonal truths give 23.19 and 24.56 km, so that change is the loss of XP's drag term as much as the alias); ISS (51.64 deg) 7.81 to 0.00 km (-100 %; 34.2 h 0.50 km, -94 %; at least 25 % lower; at 21.7 h the tess2 and zonal truths give 11.17 and 12.39 km, so that change is the loss of XP's drag term as much as the alias).

## 13. Truth packs and windows of the lunar, cislunar and heliocentric regimes (amendments A8 and A9)

Truth packs (local: `<store>/pack/`, states in km and km/s, inertial axes of the source, UTC) and the window lists (`regime-windows.json`: window starts and counts, no states) for the regimes SGP4 and SGP4-XP do not describe; the build scores them in sections 14 and 15. A window needs 90 % of its states in the fit span and in each held-out day. `regime-windows.mjs --export <pack> --start <ISO> --fit-hours <h> --out <file.oem>` writes a window's fit span as a $OEM (center and frame named); `score-pack.mjs --pack <pack> --start <ISO> --fit-hours <h> --predicted <states.oem>` scores predicted states against the held-out days of that window (radial / in-track / cross-track RMS by day, in the pack's center, through gp-error-model `pair_statistics`).

## 15. The cislunar and heliocentric packs through the segment fits (amendment A9h)

The build's segment fits, reached through the same methods (`fit` routes Sun-centered blocks, Moon-centered blocks whose first state's apocenter lies beyond 10,000 km and Earth-centered blocks whose apocenter lies beyond 250,000 km to a segment fit; `propagate` writes the set on a grid whose center is the set's own): every window of the cislunar and heliocentric packs listed in section 13 (175 windows). One $OEM block per window with the fit span and the held-out days (the fit span declared as the useable interval), no environment, no prior; the set is propagated to every held-out state of the pack, none thinned or dropped (the packs carry 1 ms time-stamp jitter, so states are paired by epoch within 5 ms), and scored by `score-pack.mjs` (gp-error-model `pair_statistics` in the pack's axes). Medians over the fitted windows, km; held-out day k is (fit end + (k-1) d, fit end + k d]. The radial / in-track / cross-track split is in `tables.json`.

### 16b. Maneuvers, the reentry hand-off, ISS and lunar maneuver detection

Build b9bc708baa50. The truths of M22, M23 and M28 are FFM-1 (propagator/ffm), not the module's own Cowell integration: impulses and finite burns planted in its typed request, synthetic decays integrated to its own stop at 100 km geodetic altitude.

Not run: M26 (the Starlink detector against the files' own states: the raw files are not kept), M33 (the thrust-onset test needs files of an orbit raise, which the captured batch has not begun) and the BeiDou part of M27 (the BeiDou orbit notices of the CSNO test and assessment center did not answer from this host; the BeiDou site's advisories are upgrade and maintenance outages, not orbit notices).

#### M22. The impulse map

48 registered Starlink truths (every fifth in NORAD order). Each is fitted as in the G1 rows (first 24 h), `maneuver` applies one impulse of the stated size at the fit end t_b, and days 1 to 3 after t_b are scored against FFM-1 restarted 60 s before t_b with the same impulse planted. The control restarts FFM-1 without an impulse and reproduces the registered truth to 0.062 m (median) and 0.145 m (largest) over the 864 samples; its day 1 / 2 / 3 median RMS is 0.155 / 0.438 / 0.980 km against 0.154 / 0.436 / 0.979 km for the no-maneuver rows of this build for the same satellites (largest difference per satellite and day 0.0066 km over 48).

#### M23. Finite burns

24 satellites (every tenth). Burns of 1 to 60 min along U, V or W at constant acceleration in the RTN axes from t_b, with total delta-v of 0.1 or 1 m/s; FFM-1 in two stages. Arm A: the module's finite-burn map (`maneuver` with EVENT_START_TIME t_b and EVENT_END_TIME t_b + D: the eccentricity-vector and plane changes scaled by sinc(dL/2)); arm B: a centroid impulse (EVENT_START_TIME t_b + D/2, no duration). #### M28. The hand-off criteria and the reentry epoch

12 synthetic decays integrated by FFM-1 to its stop at 100 km (T100), 134 nodes in all (the first sample at which the revolution-mean perigee height falls below a level of the ladder), the SDN-AP set fitted on the truth up to the node (the module chooses its span), scored on the next 12 h in four 3 h blocks, and `reentry` on the same set. eps1 is 2 |da_rev| / H with H FFM-1's NRLMSISE-00 density scale height at the perigee; the module's own H is not exposed. #### M24. The ISS ephemerides through `detect_maneuvers`

The two distinct NASA OEM files in the archive (created 2026-10-07 and 2026-10-21; 15 days each, 29 object-days together) hold no reboost: the event table of the first lists docking, departure and launch events with delta-v 0.0, the second carries none. 26 windows of 72 h (every 24 h, every 240 s state, TEME by foundation/frames): 26 answered, 0 refused (none); 0 windows reported a burn (0 detections at 0 distinct epochs; delta-v median  m/s, maximum  m/s; Lambda median ). Every detection is a false alarm: 0.0 per object-year over the distinct epochs. The detector ran with the saved space-weather table loaded (`load_environment`, amendment A10d). Detection of real reboosts and the errors of delta-v and epoch need the NASA archive of past OEM headers (TIG, DV, HA, HP), which the local archive does not hold.

Control on burn-free FFM-1 arcs (A10c): 96 windows of 72 h on 48 registered Starlink truths (one integration from one state, 300 s states): 4 windows reported a burn (4 detections at 4 distinct epochs; delta-v median 0.079 m/s, maximum 0.113 m/s; Lambda median 44.4), 7.6 per object-year over 192 object-days; refused: none.

#### M24 on NASA's ISS archive (amendment A11)

748 OEM files in the archive (NASA JSC FOD TOPO; dated folders 2020 to 2026, fetched 2026-10-24; 727 give a window), 99 distinct planned burns (header events with a delta-v above zero, TIGs within 12 h taken as one burn). TIG and delta-v are the headers' planned values, not measurements of the executed burns. Each window is 72 h, every state (TEME by foundation/frames), `detect_maneuvers` with the daily space weather of the years loaded (`load_environment`). A detection within 92 min (one orbit) of TIG is a hit; the epoch error is the detected epoch less TIG, the delta-v ratio the detected |delta-v| over the planned one (median [10th, 90th percentile] over the hits):

| pre-burn file (burn still to come) | 97 | 95 | 2 | 0 | 5.6 [0.1, 10.2] | 1.01 [0.97, 1.15] | 0.03 | 0.99 |
| post-burn file (burn behind its start) | 6 | 6 | 0 | 0 | 1.3 [-0.9, 6.9] | 1.01 [0.92, 1.05] | 0.03 | 0.99 |

#### M27. GPS and QZSS station keeping against the NANUs and NAQUs (amendments A14 and A14b)

The detector on 72 h windows every 24 h of the GPS satellites (ESA final orbits, 2026-001 to 2026-266) and the QZSS satellites (GFZ multi-GNSS rapid orbits, 2026-004 to 2026-273), against the forecast delta-V outages of the GPS NANUs (97 of 2026) and the QZSS NAQUs (261 of 2026). An outage is detected when a distinct detection (merged within 6 h) lies inside its forecast window plus or minus 6 h; the actual interval is the summary notice's. The baseline of a detection (A14b) is the off-notice window rate: the same window placed at every whole hour of the satellite's answered span where it overlaps no announced outage (plus or minus 6 h), the share of placements that hold a distinct detection; the cell gives the number of outages expected detected under those rates (the satellite's own, and the same placements pooled over the system's satellites), the number observed, and the probability of at least that many (independent outages, exact). False alarms are distinct detections outside every forecast window plus or minus 6 h of their satellite; the last two columns count those inside another outage notice's window and within 2 h of a daily-file boundary. The chance level is the share of a satellite's covered time inside its forecast windows plus or minus 6 h, the hit probability of one detection at a random epoch (A14; median over the satellites with outages). BeiDou is not scored: its notices were not reachable (see section 1).

### 16c. Real reentries, controlled deorbits and first sets (amendment A11)

#### M29. Real reentries

Build b9bc708baa50. Predictions at element-set epochs from 14 days before the reentry (4 h apart or more): the pseudo-observations of the object's element-set history up to the epoch (the last 120 h; the SGP4 module propagates each set over the interval it speaks for, 300 s states) go to `fit` (the module chooses the span) and the set to `reentry` with the daily space weather of the years. Time to go is the true one (truth less the prediction epoch); the error is REENTRY_EPOCH less the truth and sigma is REENTRY_EPOCH_UNC (68.27 %). The 20 % rule is |error| <= 0.2 (REENTRY_EPOCH less the epoch); the equal-coverage k is the multiple of sigma that covers the same share of the predictions in the bin as the rule does, and k sigma over the rule's window is below 1 where the module's window is narrower at equal coverage. The space weather is the observed daily values of the years (GFZ Kp, ap, Ap and F10.7 up to 2026-09-12, the saved table after it), which include the days after each prediction epoch: a forecast issued at the epoch would be less exact, so the comparison with Aerospace and with the TIP messages, which use forecasts, favors SDN-AP in that one respect.

#### M31. First sets of a new batch

The method is covered by the build (`launch_set` makes the set from the insertion orbit; `fit` takes an early ephemeris with that set as `prior`). The data are not held: the capture cycles keep derived products only, so the cycle of 2026-10-24T05:01Z, in which the new batch appeared (27 satellites, provisional ids 799501701 to 799501727), holds no first state, and the server serves each satellite's current file only. The clock was started with the current files (created 2026-10-24T09:01 to 2026-10-24T09:31 UTC, processed 2026-10-24T17:58 to 2026-10-24T18:18 UTC, one request at a time, kept in memory only): 27 of 27 processed as the registered Starlinks are (fit by analysis/od, FFM-1 for 96 h, derived values kept). The per-object fits of 6, 12 and 24 h inside the files' own later states follow (amendment A12b); `launch_set` and `launch_batch` need the batch's parent state, which the files do not carry, and are measured on synthetic batches below; the scoring on newer files (days 1 to 3 after the fit end) is registered in A11c for 2026-10-25 to 2026-10-28 and is not run.

The states of the captured files start 2026-10-24T08:48 to 2026-10-24T09:22 UTC. Fit of the first 24 h against SpaceX's states: RMS median 0.64 km (90th percentile 0.71); B_eff median 0.0145 m2/kg; at the zero bound: 0. Held-out RMS against the FFM-1 truth, days 1 / 2 / 3, median (km): SGP4-XP 7.7 / 21.3 / 22.6; SGP4 8.2 / 24.9 / 33.2. What M31 needs from the capture: the first state (only) of every new id kept at first sight, with the file's hash.

M29, build 61d4ec4e6a12 -> build b9bc708baa50: sample C: answered 362 of 441 -> 378 of 441, within 1 sigma 60 % -> 56 %, within 2 sigma 69 % -> 65 %, median absolute error 11.4 -> 10.8 h; sample T: answered 1357 of 1368 -> 1360 of 1368, within 1 sigma 56 % -> 49 %, within 2 sigma 83 % -> 75 %, median absolute error 15.4 -> 14.6 h.

M30, build 61d4ec4e6a12 -> build b9bc708baa50: `reentry` answered 17 -> 19 of 19 at the shipped setting (predicted lifetime median 12.5 -> 12.4 days), and 0 -> 19 at the rule setting (none refused); both builds' predictions are frozen in the rows with their issue times.

## Reproduce, resume, extend

- Steps (each reads only the frozen plan and the local archive): `population.mjs`, `truth.mjs --workers N` (resumable: a satellite with a row is skipped; stop with `touch <store>/truth.stop`), `baselines.mjs`, `fetch-kernels.mjs` (paced NAIF fetcher) with `spk-windows.mjs`, `bench.mjs`, `comspoc.mjs`, `covariance.mjs`, `fitlength.mjs`, `refit.mjs`, `newer.mjs` (re-run as newer SpaceX files arrive; `--truth refit30` scores the refitted truth), `m123.mjs --workers N` with `m123-report.mjs` (section 12), `fetch-horizons.mjs`, `fetch-mast-tess.mjs` (needs `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, see its header), `fetch-gnss-rapid.mjs`, `fetch-slr.mjs` (needs the Earthdata token in the environment file `lib/cddis.mjs` names, never printed) with `gnss-rapid-convert.mjs` and `slr-convert.mjs` (the public sources approved on 2026-10-24, stored in the archive with provenance rows), `sdngp-run.mjs`, `span-windows.mjs` (the SGP4 and XP rows of section 1c), `horizons-packs.mjs`, `regime-windows.mjs` and `score-pack.mjs` (section 13), `watch-newer.mjs` (waits for the next SpaceX batch, runs `newer.mjs`, regenerates the tables, commits nothing), `inventory.mjs`, `tables.mjs`, `manifest.mjs`; the E2E is `node experiments/sdn-gp-starlink-ffm-match/run.mjs --check`. Setup: `npm ci --prefix experiments/sdn-gp-starlink-ffm-match`; a modules checkout at commit 118de023 (sparse is enough: propagator/sgp4-xp without third_party, propagator/ffm, analysis/od, analysis/gp-error-model, foundation, data-source/space-weather, data) named by `SDN_MODULES_ROOT`, with its `node_modules` linked to the experiment's.
- Local store (not committed): `/opt/data/sdn-archive/sdn-gp-starlink-ffm-match/` holds the derived FFM trajectories (`truth/`), the saved environment (EOP, space weather with its extension) and the window rows.
- SDN-AP: `lib/sdngp.mjs` runs side B's `sdn-ap-propagator` (named `sdn-gp-propagator` through build 35cd83a2b714; a copy of its built wasm, named by SHA-256 prefix, in `<store>/sdngp-snapshot/`; a new build is read from a commit of the public modules repo, `propagator/sdn-ap` (the closed-modules tree before modules commit c0c5da38), by `git show`: `SDNAP_MODULES_REF`, default origin/main), `lib/baselines.mjs` `fitSdngp` is the third fitter beside `fitXp` and `fitSgp4`, and `sdngp-run.mjs` runs it over every source on one build (`baselines.mjs --arm sdngp`, `spk-windows.mjs --arm sdngp`, `starlink-sdngp.mjs`, `starlink-lowering.mjs`, `fitlength.mjs --arm sdngp`, `span-windows.mjs --arm sdngp`; `newer.mjs` carries the arm when a complete build exists), rows in `<store>/rows/sdngp/<build>/`; a new build of the module is one more `sdngp-run.mjs`, and section 2a lists the builds. The last row of section 5 stays `pending`. For the regimes awaiting SDN-AP, `pack-truth.mjs` writes the truth packs and `compareStates` (gp-error-model `pair_statistics`) scores any predicted states against them in radial / in-track / cross-track axes whatever the center. The arms 3-6 of the task (term ranking, Chebyshev residual bytes, analytic STM) follow in phases 3-4.
- Maneuvers, hand-off and detection (amendment A10): `events-truth.mjs --kind m22,m23,m28 --shard k/n` makes the FFM-1 truths (impulses and finite burns planted in its typed request; synthetic decays to its stop at 100 km) under `<store>/events/`, independent of the build; `sdngp-run.mjs --pin <build> --only m22,m23,m28,m24` scores them with that build of the module (rows `m22`, `m23`, `m28`, `m24` in `rows/sdngp/<build>/`); `lib/sdngp-events.mjs` is the module's `maneuver`, `detect_maneuvers` and `reentry` surfaces, `lib/ffm-events.mjs` and `lib/decay.mjs` are FFM-1's.
- NASA ISS archive, reentries and first sets (amendments A11 to A12b): `fetch-iss-oem.mjs` fetches NASA's public ISS OEM archive (748 files, 2 s apart, nothing committed) and `iss-burns.mjs` writes `iss-burns.json` from the headers alone; `m29-extract.mjs` and `m29-extract-cords.mjs` cut the element-set histories of the reentered objects from the local Space-Track archive and the CORDS spreadsheet (kept in the archive, never committed); `m30-truth.mjs` writes `m30-truth.json` (re-run it, then `tables.mjs`, as TIP and decay messages arrive: the frozen reentry predictions of the lowering Starlinks are then scored); `m31-capture.mjs` processes the current files of a new batch into derived trajectories (`--summary` writes `m31-capture.json`); `m32-truth.mjs` makes the synthetic batches' FFM-1 truths; `sdngp-run.mjs --pin <build> --only m24a,m24p,m29,m30,m31,m32` scores them with a build of the module.
- GPS and QZSS station keeping and the next build (amendments A14 to A15): `fetch-gnss-notices.mjs` fetches the 2026 GPS NANUs (NAVCEN) and the QZSS NAQU page, 5 s apart, into the local archive (`hac/gnss-notices/`; no notice text is committed), `m27-events.mjs` writes the outage windows to `gnss-notice-events.json`, and `sdngp-run.mjs --pin <build> --only m27` scores the detector on the GPS (ESA final) and QZSS (GFZ rapid) orbit series (`lib/events-gnss.mjs`). `lib/pcf.mjs` encodes the $PCF `configuration` record that builds from b9bc708baa50 on require (SDS 1.264.0 as `spacedatastandards-1264`; every other record stays on 1.261.0). A build that is still running its sources is not read; it joins the tables when `rows/sdngp/<build>/events-focus.json` names the sources it has run and counts their rows (written by hand after their jobs exit 0, as `complete.json` is for a full run), and only those sources are read until the build is complete.
- Newer-file truth: `newer.mjs` scores against the first hours of any SpaceX file created after the registered fit (section 10); re-run it after 2026-10-24 08:00Z for the registered prospective files, and `comspoc.mjs` against the same-day truth files. GPS and laser-ranged objects of the COMSPOC capture need the ESA and ILRS products for 2026-10-23 and later (ESA finals lag about two weeks); `inventory.mjs` shows the newest product dates.
